# Before and after

Every change that moves speed, memory or accuracy gets a row here with the numbers before and after, measured the
same way, and the check that the answers did not change. Newest first. The long story is in
[review.md](review.md) and [speed-progress.md](speed-progress.md); what each finding taught is in
[lessons-intel-arc-igpu.md](lessons-intel-arc-igpu.md).

**Test machine:** Intel Core Ultra 5 125H (Arc iGPU, 7 Xe-cores / 112 EUs, no XMX), 32 GB LPDDR5x-6400, Kingston
NVMe SSD (3.5-4.3 GB/s for 1-4 MB reads), Windows 11, nothing else on the GPU. Pauses between runs (the laptop
slows down by 5-10 % when hot); one run per cell unless noted. tok/s = tokens per second.

**How "answers did not change" is checked:** the logits of every prompt position are dumped (`runz --dump-logits`)
and compared byte for byte with the run before the change, or the generated greedy text is compared character for
character. "Bit-identical" below means exactly that, not "close". Changes that are allowed to change the numbers
(new quantization, 4-bit V) are measured as KL divergence against a reference instead.

## Where it stands

(Updated 2026-10-25; each row's date and measurement are in the rows below.)

| Model | Prompt | Generation | Accuracy |
|---|---|---|---|
| Qwen3.6 35B-A3B UD-IQ4_XS (MoE, experts on the SSD) | 604 tok/s (32K prompt), 324 tok/s (128K) | 20.6 tok/s; Python code 49.9 with DFlash2, Indonesian 21.9 with MTP; 12.6 at 128K depth | KL 0.004-0.008 against an f32 reference (generation path) |

| Qwen3.8 Flash Next (IQ3_S, 512 experts on the SSD) | ~164 tok/s (4K, 2026-10-23), 163 (32K), 123 (125K) | 6-7 tok/s | KL 0.0143 prompt / 0.0147 decode on 363 tokens of README text; 0.0186 / 0.0177 on a 3,584-token prompt (QSA sparse); `ARC_FN_PRECISE=1` (opt-in, about half the prompt speed) 0.0127 / 0.0132 |

Two conversations at once (`--slots 2`), both writing their answers, their next tokens in one engine pass (tok/s of
the two together): 27B prose 7.3 -> 10.5, code 11.6 -> 15.4; 35B 20.2 -> 24.7, 29.0 -> 30.7; Gemma 4 26B 19.8 -> 26.2
(taking turns -> batched; logits bit-identical to each slot alone).

|---|---|---|---|
| Qwen3.5 4B | 911 / 242 / 337 tok/s (**2.7-3.8x**) | 57.5 s / 376 s / 163 s | 15.7 / 10.6 / 8.2 tok/s |
| Qwen3.5 9B | 402 / 151 / 189 | 96 s / 461 s / 243 s | 8.6 / 7.3 / 6.4 |
| Qwen3.8 27B | 113 / 23 / 25 (**4.5-4.9x**) | 327 s / 2,418 s / 1,624 s | 3.7 / 2.1 / 1.1 |

## Accuracy, consistency and answer quality

**Accuracy against a full-precision reference.** `tools/ref_forward` is an independent f32 CPU implementation of
each model; the KL divergence of the next-token distributions (0 = identical, averaged over the prompt positions)
measures how far the int8 GPU arithmetic moves the model:

| Model | KL vs f32 | What it means |
|---|---:|---|
| Qwen3.6 35B-A3B (GSQ) | 0.0008 | the int8 engine and the f32 reference pick the same tokens with nearly the same probabilities |
| Qwen3.5 4B | 0.0021 | 0.0006 of it is int8 attention |
| Qwen3.8 27B (generation path) | 0.0003-0.0004 | run-to-run rounding noise is of the same size |
| Qwen3.6 35B-A3B UD-IQ4_XS (generation path) | 0.004-0.008 | a small change in attention can pick other experts |
| Gemma 4 12B (QAT, 480-token code prompt) | 0.0002 | Gemma 4 is ~7x more sensitive to int8 activations: float attention and two int8 passes (value + rounding residual); one pass gave 0.021 |
| Gemma 4 26B-A4B (QAT) | 0.0042 | two passes; one pass gave 0.0985 |
| Gemma 4 E4B (QAT) | 0.00088 | one pass is enough here |
| Qwen3.8 Flash Next (363 tokens / 3.5K prompt, QSA) | 0.0143 / 0.0186 | the most sensitive model; `ARC_FN_PRECISE=1` 0.0117 / 0.0127 at about half the prompt speed |
| Qwen3-ASR 1.7B text model (Qwen3 dense, 72-token prompt) | < 0.00001 | int8 attention gave 0.236; float attention with one int8 pass 0.0064 / 0.0093 (decode); limit for this model 0.01 |

**Perplexity against llama.cpp** (40 chunks of llama.cpp documentation, lower is better): Qwen3.5 4B 6.056 vs
6.141 (Vulkan), 9B 5.417 vs 5.499, 27B 4.274 vs 4.311. Speed did not cost quality: on the same GGUF files
arcinferzero's int8 path scores as well as llama.cpp's.

**Consistency.** The engine is deterministic: the same prompt gives the same logits, bit for bit, run after run.
That property is what every speed change is held to:

- arcinferzero (Level Zero) gave bit-identical logits to the OpenCL engine on every model, checked until that engine
  was removed (2026-10-20); since then every change is checked against the previous build.
- Speculative decoding (MTP block, DFlash2, both) gives exactly the text of plain decoding; a drift after ~40
  tokens on the 35B was found this way and fixed (the verification rows took another attention kernel).
- The SSD expert streaming gives the same logits whatever the cache size (41 % or 97 % of the 35B's experts,
  13-23 % of Flash Next's) and whether the GPU runs out of memory midway (the cache gives back memory in 1 GiB
  pieces): 56 of 56 regression runs identical, including 40 Flash Next prompts in a row.
- Tokenization equals `llama-tokenize`; chat templates render exactly like the GGUF's Jinja templates.

## Changes, newest first

- **2026-11-18, `ARC_TP_EVSUM=1` (opt-in): generation's split sums on GPU events:** the laptop, Qwen3.5 4B over 2
  shards (`ARC_TP_SIM`, on 2 queues), logits of every generated token against the host sums: a 48-token answer 6 / 6
  runs 901D5D90... both ways; `USAGE.md` (18.6K tokens, the reduce-scatter, then event sums) + 8 tokens 52771C66... both
  ways, twice; 200 generated tokens (the event ring wraps ~13 times) AD3AEC83... both ways, twice; 3 shards on 2 queues:
  the switch off, as it says, 4EBE5A8D... both ways. The first form (each event reset just before its reuse) gave 1 run
  in 3 other logits: fixed by the reset per half ring behind a sync and a barrier. Speed: two real GPUs to measure (the
  simulated shards share one GPU).

- **2026-11-18, Alchemist: the preset over resident weights when `--budget` is not given:** an Arc A770 (hansong80,
  issue #35, release .02), Qwen3.5 4B UD-Q4_K_XL 4K prompt: resident DP4a at chunk 4096 3,575-3,605 tok/s (7.40 GiB),
  `--budget 0` (the preset) 4,819.6 / 4,820.8 (3.98 GiB); generation 38.17 / 37.80 tok/s. The same card: 9B
  `ARC_GEMM_XW_WGX=1` 2,927-2,940 against WGX 2's 3,069-3,071 (+4.4 %), split 34 2,990-3,000 against 17's (+2.4 %),
  chunk 8192 3,063-3,067 (no gain, +1.2 GiB); 27B one card 4K 1,177, generation 4.63 tok/s (52.5 GB/s, 19 %); over
  both cards 1,126 / 11.75 tok/s (no peer access: 71.7 of each 79 ms token waits for the host sums). KL, the laptop's
  4B, the emulated preset against resident DP4a: 0.0039 (max 4.37, same top 97.1 %).

- **2026-11-18, Alchemist: `h8_splits` 17 superblocks a piece (34 before):** an Arc A770 (TheUniKian, issue #36,
  release .08), 27B IQ3_S 4K prompt, best of 3: default (34) 1,150.0 tok/s (`gemm_down` 822.9 ms, 128 launches),
  `ARC_GEMM_SPLIT=0` 1,154.7 (815.1, 64), `=17` 1,222.7 (622.9, 256), `ARC_GEMM_XW_WGX=4` 1,158.3 (down 853.0, up
  550 -> 523, gate 486 -> 452). KL, the laptop's emulated 140T preset (`ARC_XMX_TRY=2 ARC_140T=1`), `USAGE.md` 2 x
  4,096 tokens: 17 against 34 pieces mean 0.00110, max 0.1501, same top 98.46 % (perplexity 6.1688 / 6.1800). The
  laptop's own default is 17 already: unchanged.

- **2026-11-18, measured: `ARC_GEMM_XW_2D` on a B60 under Linux** (MaineTim, issue #19, main 1b5bd69), 27B
  UD-Q5_K_XL 4K prompt, 2 rounds alternating: 2D 1,644.9 / 1,645.7 tok/s, `ARC_GEMM_XW_2D=0` 1,576.4 / 1,580.3 (+4.1 %;
  the B70 +4.6 %). An Arc A770 (TheUniKian, issue #36, release .08, the 140T preset): 27B IQ3_S 4K 1,140.7, 9B 3,042.1,
  Gemma 4 12B 671.7 tok/s (DP4a: the XMX check lost, 2.4 vs 4.9 ms); its 27B `gemm_down` 825 ms against `gemm_up`'s

- **2026-11-19, the 4,096-token chunks of resident weights reach 12 GB cards (self audit):** the line was 12 GiB,

  of the row below (3,211-3,219 / 3,594-3,598 tok/s) is what to expect; the laptop's KL 0.00000 between the two

- **2026-11-15, the single-token GEMV's window reads one window ahead, opt-in** (`ARC_GEMV_DB=1`, decode.cl
  `-DGEMV_DB`, zero/xe2/engine_xe2.h; after urakozz's GEMV, whose loads stay in flight across its dequantization).
  `gemv` (IQ3_S, Q4_K, Q5_K, Q8_0, ...) loads the next row's or the next step's window into registers before it
  decodes the current one; in place of `ARC_GEMV_PF`. Laptop, `runz --bench-pp 512`, off and on alternated: Qwen3.5 4B
  Q4_K_XL `--n 128` 17.49 / 19.66 / 19.94 / 20.23 -> 18.63 / 19.88 / 19.95 / 20.30 tok/s (within noise; `gemm_gate`
  876.8 -> 875.8 ms over 128 tokens); 27B IQ3_S `--n 32` 4.77 / 4.70 -> 4.84 / 4.65. **No gain on the laptop**, and
  none was expected there: the probes (`ARC_GV_PROBE`, now for every type on the `gemv` path) show the 4B's GEMVs
  bound by memory (no decode at all: `gemm_gate` 877 -> 900 ms, not faster; gate / up already ~64 GB/s) and the
  27B's IQ3_S layers by their decoding (decode only, no streaming: `gemm_gate` 1,273 -> 1,226 ms, `gemm_down` 1,309
  -> 1,215, though only a quarter to a third of the layers take the `gemv` path). Speed on B-series cards: not
  measured. Logits bit-identical (`--dump-logits`, a 21-token prompt: 4B + 64 generated tokens, 27B + 24); the
  default build's code is unchanged. ocloc `bmg` and `mtl-h`: IQ3_S (2 rows), Q4_K (4), Q5_K and Q8_0 (1) build, no
  spills in `gemv`.

- **2026-11-09, Qwen's reasoning: line breaks free of its presence penalty** (`tools/server_chat.h`,
  `tools/sampler.h`). Sampling only, logits unchanged (no KL). Measured on the 35B chat where "ctlsc" appeared: the
  exact prompt up to the stray token, the server's top-20 log-probabilities. Before: the penalty of 1.5 hit "\n\n"
  (56.7 % -> 48.6 %) and "\n" (33.5 % -> 28.7 %), both used earlier in the reasoning, and raised "ctl" from 4.4 % to
  ~17 %. After: the 422 tokens of spaces / line breaks alone (35B vocab) keep their logits, so "ctl" stays near the
  model's own ~4 %; word tokens are still penalized. Only when the request sets no presence_penalty; Gemma
  unchanged. `test_sampler`: ALL OK, with three new checks.

  sum and GPU, in f32 part sizes: 4 GPUs 5 -> 1.1 (4.4x fewer), 2 GPUs 3 -> 0.75 (4x). Speed: to be measured on the 4x
  B60 (before: 35B prompt 3,232 tok/s on 1 GPU, 2,040-2,122 on 2-4). Accuracy, ARC_TP_SIM on the laptop, 1,687-token
  prompt + 8 tokens against one GPU: 27B KL mean 0.0016 (2 GPUs) / 0.0018 (4), max 0.0035, before 0.0012 / 0.0009;
  greedy text the same. One GPU: bit-identical to the build before.

- **2026-11-02, dense prompt GEMM with 128 registers per thread on discrete GPUs** (measured by the owner of 4x Arc
  Pro B60, issue #4; one B60, Qwen3.8 27B IQ3_S, 4,096-token prompt, best of 3): 449.7 -> 617.5 tok/s (+37 %); the XMX
  check passed. DP4a only: 406.5. The MoE expert GEMMs keep 256 (`ARC_MOE_GRF=128` to measure). iGPUs, with XMX or
  not, keep 256: on the 125H (35B, forced on with `ARC_XMX_TRY`) 128 gave 519 -> 194 tok/s, and 148 with the MoE
  GEMMs at 128 too. Laptop default: the same kernel builds as before, logits bit-identical. Same B60, Qwen3.6
  35B-A3B (`--gpus N`, before this change): prompt 3,232 / 2,076 / 2,122 / 2,040 tok/s on 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 GPUs, 65-80
  % of the split runs waiting on the sums through host memory; generation 77.5 / 92.2 / 95.3 / 101.7 tok/s.

- **2026-11-01, multi-GPU MoE (Qwen3.6 35B-A3B) and experts in RAM.** One GPU: logits bit-identical to the build
  before (1,687-token prompt + 32 tokens; full cache, and 1.2 GiB at 50 % hits); speed best of two 608 vs 612 tok/s
  prompt, 21.6 vs 21.8 tok/s generation (noise). Split (ARC_TP_SIM, one iGPU: speeds mean nothing): KL to one GPU
  over the first 4 positions 0.007-0.023, as one GPU's own between chunk sizes 512 and 8192 (0.0026-0.017). RAM
  tier forced on the laptop (4 GiB cache): bit-identical; generation 13.1 vs 13.3 tok/s, prompt 275 vs 372 tok/s
  (off by default on an iGPU). Flash Next after its re-download, the same 1,687-token prompt: bit-identical to the
  build before in the default and precise modes; default 98.1 (before) vs 93.4-97.0 tok/s prompt, 6.19 vs 6.15-6.37
  tok/s generation; precise 54.4 vs 55.5-59.6 / 5.28 vs 5.20-5.21.

### What did not pay off (kept out on purpose)

- Line breaks free of Qwen's reasoning presence penalty (2026-11-09): it kept the model's stray "ctlsc" at its own
  4.4 % instead of ~17 %, but the 35B then looped in short indented lines without closing its reasoning.
- Larger expert staging by default (`ARC_XSTAGE=256`): prompts +5-11 % but generation -2-3 % (host RAM taken from
  the cache). Deep prefetch gets the overlap without it.
- GEMM tile / split-K / work-group autotune: the defaults were already the fastest on 27B and 35B.
- 35B expert decoding once per layer across chunks: ~1 GB of extra buffers would come out of the expert cache;
  8192-token chunks give most of the gain.
- Faster attention variants for Qwen prompts (`attn_ps`, other tilings): slower than `attn_q8` with 256 registers.
- **Dense prompt GEMM straight from the raw rows, DP4a (2026-11-08):** `decode_w8` fused into the GEMM. Each
  work-group decoded its 16 weight rows once per 512 tokens into local memory, then ran `gemm.cl`'s dot products on
  them.
  - The aim: on the 125H, `decode_w8` takes 1.1 s of a 4.6 s 512-token 27B prompt, a fixed cost per chunk.
  - Bit-identical to decode + GEMM (2,161 tokens in 512-token chunks).
  - Not faster: `--bench-pp 512` 106-110 against 110 tok/s, and 2,161 tokens in 512-token chunks 100.7 against 122.1.
    `decode_w8` fell to 275 ms, but gate / up / down took 24-33 % longer.
  - Why: the GEMM already runs at about half the iGPU's DP4a peak (gate: ~3.9 T int8 MAC/s), and `decode_w8` is
    vector work too (codebook lookups), not memory traffic. Fused, the decoding takes the same vector units, so
    nothing is saved. The 140T's DP4a chunks are in the same position.
  - Where it could pay: an XMX version (`gemm.cl -DXMX`, with H8's rotation), where DPAS runs on the matrix engine
    while the vector units decode. Not built; only a B-series card can measure it.
- More in [lessons-intel-arc-igpu.md](lessons-intel-arc-igpu.md#5-tried-and-not-kept).

## Adding a row

Measure before and after on the same build options with a pause in between, best of two when a single number is
noisy, and state the check: bit-identical logits (`runz --dump-logits A.bin` vs `B.bin`, `cmp`), identical greedy
text, or KL against the f32 reference / the int8 cache (`runz --ppl-kl-save` / `--ppl-kl`) for changes that are
meant to change the numbers.
